# A JUMP-MODIFIED WASSERSTEIN GRADIENT FLOW FOR MAXIMUM MEAN DISCREPANCY WITH ENERGY KERNELS

ANSH DESAI<sub>,</sub> JASON LIU<sub>,</sub> SRUJAN ROPLEKAR<sub>,</sub> AND MATTHEW ROSENZWEIG

Abstract. We study a jump-modified Wasserstein gradient flow of the squared maximum mean discrepancy generated by the energy kernels $K ( z ) = - | z | ^ { q } , 0 < q < 2$ , in every dimension � $\geq 2$ as well as the one-dimensional ran<sub>g</sub>e $1 \leq q < 2$ . Under subcritical densit<sub>y</sub> and finite-moment assum<sub>p</sub>tions<sub>,</sub> we prove global well-posedness. The added jumps transfer mass toward smaller values of the first variation and dissi<sub>p</sub>ate the same MMD ener<sub>gy</sub> as the trans<sub>p</sub>ort term. Under our h<sub>yp</sub>otheses on the tar<sub>g</sub>et and jump rates, every Borel probability measure with finite MMD energy and zero jump dissipation is e<sub>q</sub>ual to the tar<sub>g</sub>et. Combinin<sub>g</sub> this ri<sub>g</sub>idit<sub>y</sub> with com<sub>p</sub>actness and a second-moment identit<sub>y y</sub>ields conver<sub>g</sub>ence to the tar<sub>g</sub>et from finite-second-moment data<sub>,</sub> without additional uniform-in-time densit<sub>y</sub> or moment assum<sub>p</sub>tions. We also construct a <sub>g</sub>lobal collision-free <sub>p</sub>article <sub>p</sub>rocess whose ener<sub>gy</sub> is nonincreasin<sub>g</sub> alon<sub>g</sub> each <sub>p</sub>ath. A <sub>q</sub>uantitative mean-field estimate a<sub>pp</sub>lies to <sub>g</sub>eneral initial em<sub>p</sub>irical a<sub>pp</sub>roximations<sub>,</sub> includin<sub>g</sub> deterministic confi<sub>g</sub>urations<sub>; p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ation of chaos for exchan<sub>g</sub>eable data and long-time target approximation follow as consequences.

## 1. Introduction

Maximum mean discrepancy (MMD) is a kernel-based discrepancy between probability distribu-<sup>ti</sup>ons, w<sup>ith</sup> app<sup>li</sup>ca<sup>ti</sup>ons <sup>t</sup>o <sup>t</sup>wo-samp<sup>l</sup>e <sup>t</sup>es<sup>ti</sup>ng, <sup>di</sup>s<sup>t</sup>r<sup>ib</sup>u<sup>ti</sup>on approx<sup>i</sup>ma<sup>ti</sup>on, an<sup>d</sup> genera<sup>ti</sup>ve <sup>l</sup>earn<sup>i</sup>ng [Gre+12; Arb+19; Her+24b]. Its Wasserstein gradient flow transports a source distribution toward a <sub>p</sub>rescribed tar<sub>g</sub>et. In a<sub>pp</sub>lications<sub>,</sub> the tar<sub>g</sub>et is often accessible onl<sub>y</sub> throu<sub>g</sub>h sam<sub>p</sub>les<sub>,</sub> and kernel avera<sub>g</sub>es can then be evaluated a<sub>g</sub>ainst its em<sub>p</sub>irical measure. The anal<sub>y</sub>tical <sub>q</sub>uestion is whether the resultin<sub>g</sub> evolution actuall<sub>y</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>roaches the tar<sub>g</sub>et<sub>,</sub> rather than merel<sub>y</sub> decreasin<sub>g</sub> the discre<sub>p</sub>anc<sub>y</sub>.

We consider the Euclidean ener<sub>gy</sub> kernels

$$
K ( z ) = - | z | ^ { q } , \qquad 0 < q < 2 ,\tag{1.1}
$$

and add nonlocal jumps to the corresponding Wasserstein gradient flow. The transport part moves the density along the negative gradient of the first variation. The jump part instead transfers mass from lar<sub>g</sub>er to smaller values of that first variation<sub>,</sub> includin<sub>g</sub> between <sub>p</sub>oints that are far a<sub>p</sub>art. Both mechanisms dissi<sub>p</sub>ate the same MMD ener<sub>gy</sub>. Their zero-dissi<sub>p</sub>ation conditions are diferent: vanishin<sub>g</sub> trans<sub>p</sub>ort dissi<sub>p</sub>ation onl<sub>y</sub> tests the <sub>g</sub>radient where the source carries mass<sub>,</sub> whereas vanishing jump dissipation compares those points with every possible destination.

This distinction is the main idea of the <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er. We <sub>p</sub>rove that an<sub>y</sub> Borel <sub>p</sub>robabilit<sub>y</sub> measure with finite MMD energy and zero jump dissipation must be the target. We then obtain convergence of the continuum evolution from finite-second-moment data<sub>,</sub> without additional uniform-in-time densit<sub>y</sub> or moment assum<sub>p</sub>tions. We also construct a collision-free <sub>p</sub>article <sub>p</sub>rocess and <sub>p</sub>rove a <sub>q</sub>uantitative mean-field estimate for <sub>g</sub>eneral initial em<sub>p</sub>irical a<sub>pp</sub>roximations<sub>,</sub> includin<sub>g</sub> deterministic confi<sub>g</sub>urations. The estimate <sub>y</sub>ields <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ation of chaos for exchan<sub>g</sub>eable initial confi<sub>g</sub>urations satisfying the stated approximation condition.

1.4. Prior and related work. Several works analyze MMD gradient flows without the jump modification considered here. Arbel, Korba, Salim, and Gretton [Arb+19] study smooth-kernel MMD flows and suficient conditions for convergence, including noise injection. For energy kernels, Hertrich, Gräf, Beinert, and Steidl [Her+24b] study Wasserstein steepest descent using tangent plans. Earlier, Di Francesco, Fornasier, Hütter, and Matthes [Di +14] studied one-dimensional <sub>p</sub>ower-law attraction–re<sub>p</sub>ulsion flows throu<sub>g</sub>h inverse distribution functions. For the distance kernel<sub>,</sub> <sup>th</sup>ey prove<sup>d</sup> convergence <sup>t</sup>o <sup>th</sup>e <sup>t</sup>arge<sup>t i</sup>n $W _ { 2 }$ for bounded<sub>,</sub> com<sub>p</sub>actl<sub>y</sub> su<sub>pp</sub>orted initial and tar<sub>g</sub>et probability densities. More recently, Duong, Stein, Beinert, Hertrich, and Steidl [Duo+26] analyze the one-dimensional distance-kernel flow throu<sub>g</sub>h <sub>q</sub>uantiles<sub>;</sub> related one-dimensional interaction flows and entro<sub>py</sub> solutions are treated in $\left[ \mathrm { B o n } + 1 5 \right]$ . Rux, Quellmalz, and Steidl [RQS26] instead smooth the distance kernel while <sub>p</sub>reservin<sub>g</sub> conditional <sub>p</sub>ositive semidefiniteness and its lar<sub>g</sub>e-scale behavior. Our e<sub>q</sub>uation uses the ori<sub>g</sub>inal nonsmooth kernel and modifies the evolution throu<sub>g</sub>h jumps.

<sup>R</sup>ecen<sup>t</sup> wor<sup>k</sup>s concern comp<sup>l</sup>emen<sup>t</sup>ary pure-<sup>t</sup>ranspor<sup>t</sup> ques<sup>ti</sup>ons. <sup>F</sup>ornas<sup>i</sup>er, <sup>H</sup>uang, an<sup>d S</sup>un [FHS26] consider power-law attraction–repulsion equations in which the exponents of the source and tar<sub>g</sub>et interactions<sub>,</sub> and their total masses<sub>,</sub> need not coincide. Their confinement results concern dominance of attraction<sub>,</sub> rather than the e<sub>q</sub>ual-ex<sub>p</sub>onent<sub>,</sub> e<sub>q</sub>ual-mass MMD settin<sub>g</sub>. Chizat<sub>,</sub> Colombo<sub>,</sub> Colombo, and Fernández-Real [Chi+26] study kernel discrepancies on the torus through negative Sobolev norms. Their <sub>q</sub>uantitative results distin<sub>g</sub>uish ex<sub>p</sub>onential conver<sub>g</sub>ence in the Coulomb case from local polynomial convergence for more regular discrepancies. Lin and Rigollet [LR26] study <sub>p</sub>ure-trans<sub>p</sub>ort flows on closed manifolds for $C ^ { \overline { { 2 } } }$ kernels dia<sub>g</sub>onal in a La<sub>p</sub>lace ei<sub>g</sub>enbasis. When the nonconstant s<sub>p</sub>ectral coeficients are <sub>p</sub>ositive<sub>,</sub> the<sub>y</sub> obtain conver<sub>g</sub>ence to uniform volume and $o ( t ^ { - 1 } )$ deca<sub>y</sub> of the ener<sub>gy</sub> <sub>g</sub>a<sub>p</sub> for initial <sub>p</sub>robabilit<sub>y</sub> densities in $L ^ { p } , p > 1$ , us<sup>i</sup>ng en<sup>t</sup>ropy <sup>di</sup>ss<sup>i</sup>pa<sup>ti</sup>on without a <sub>p</sub>ositive lower bound on the densit<sub>y</sub>. The <sub>p</sub>resent <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er treats a Euclidean e<sub>q</sub>uation with an additional nonlocal jump dissipation; neither its measure-level rigidity nor its particle jump a<sub>pp</sub>roximation follows from those trans<sub>p</sub>ort results.

The work [RSW26] provides the closest analytical comparison. It provides the nonsmooth MMD trans<sub>p</sub>ort theor<sub>y,</sub> the natural-moment ri<sub>g</sub>idit<sub>y</sub> of absolutel<sub>y</sub> continuous critical <sub>p</sub>oints<sub>,</sub> and the modulated-ener<sub>gy</sub> commutator used here. For $1 \leq q < 2$ <sub>,</sub> it <sub>p</sub>roves as<sub>y</sub>m<sub>p</sub>totic criticalit<sub>y</sub> without additional lon<sub>g</sub>-time bounds. $\operatorname { A t } q = 1$ <sub>,</sub> in dimensions at least two<sub>,</sub> finite initial and tar<sub>g</sub>et second moments <sub>g</sub>ive com<sub>p</sub>actness in ever<sub>y</sub> Wasserstein distance of order at least one and below two and e<sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> between the ener<sub>gy</sub> of each subse<sub>q</sub>uential limit and the limitin<sub>g</sub> ener<sub>gy</sub>. Tar<sub>g</sub>et identification still re<sub>q</sub>uires excludin<sub>g</sub> sin<sub>g</sub>ular critical limits<sub>;</sub> uniform $L ^ { p }$ control is a suficient additional condition [RSW26, Theorem 5.23, Theorem 1.7, and Corollary 5.27]. Chodron de Courcel and the fourth author [CR26] study Coulomb discrepancies, using the special structure of the Coulomb potential as the fundamental solution of −∆ (and its zero-mean periodic analogue) to obtain density regularization and convergence results that depend on the geometry and tar<sub>g</sub>et assum<sub>p</sub>tions. Their <sub>p</sub>ositive-order overla<sub>p</sub> with the <sub>p</sub>resent ener<sub>gy</sub>-kernel famil<sub>y</sub> is the end<sub>p</sub>oint $d = q = 1$ <sub>,</sub> while in dimensions at least two the Coulomb kernels lie outside that famil<sub>y</sub>.

We also mention static empirical-quantization results, such as [FH16; Col+26]. These concern <sub>g</sub>loball<sub>y</sub> o<sub>p</sub>timal confi<sub>g</sub>urations for the discrete ener<sub>gy</sub> associated with the <sub>p</sub>ure-trans<sub>p</sub>ort <sub>p</sub>article s<sub>y</sub>stem. The<sub>y</sub> do not im<sub>p</sub>l<sub>y</sub> that ever<sub>y</sub> evolvin<sub>g p</sub>article confi<sub>g</sub>uration reaches the o<sub>p</sub>timal <sub>q</sub>uantization error.

More recently, Lee and Ghosal [LG26] obtained quantitative particle approximations for the <sub>p</sub>eriodic Coulomb discre<sub>p</sub>anc<sub>y</sub> flow in dimensions at least two<sub>,</sub> uniforml<sub>y</sub> in time and tem<sub>p</sub>erature (including the deterministic case), under suficiently regular, strictly positive initial and target densities and inde<sub>p</sub>endent initial sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub>.

The directed mass transfers in (1.9) are closely related to the upwind nonlocal interaction equation of [Esp+21]. Weighting each directed transfer by its departure mass distinguishes that construction from the s<sub>y</sub>mmetric avera<sub>g</sub>es of the densities at both end<sub>p</sub>oints used in the reversible Markov-chain and nonlocal Wasserstein geometries of [Maa11; Erb14]; see also [SW23] for metric and locallimit <sub>q</sub>uestions. We use the de<sub>p</sub>arture mass and the <sub>p</sub>ositive diference of first variations<sub>,</sub> and add continuous s<sub>p</sub>atial trans<sub>p</sub>ort. Under the construction h<sub>yp</sub>otheses of Section 4.1<sub>,</sub> the corres<sub>p</sub>ondin<sub>g</sub> particle process is a piecewise-deterministic Markov process in the sense ofDavis [Dav84], determined by its deterministic flow, total jump rate, and post-jump kernel.

For the mean-field limit, classical approaches such as Dobrushin’s coupling estimate [Dob79] do not apply directly in their standard globally Lipschitz-force form, since ∇� is not Lipschitz at the origin. Singular forces stemming from a potential that generates a coercive energy can instead be com<sub>p</sub>ared throu<sub>g</sub>h a modulated ener<sub>gy</sub> and its trans<sub>p</sub>ort commutator<sub>,</sub> when the corres<sub>p</sub>ondin<sub>g</sub> commutator estimate is available. The Coulomb and Riesz results in [Ser20; RS23; RS26] require the stated regularity of the limiting density and velocity, with diagonal subtraction and finite-� corrections in particle comparisons. The energy-kernel commutator used here is provided by [RSW26]. Coupling jump systems with independent nonlinear processes driven by the same Poisson measures is an established strategy [ADF18]. Here the combination is essential: the modulated energy controls the singular drift, while the bounded coupling cost also handles the jump mismatch. Appendix B rules out the <sub>p</sub>articular linear ener<sub>gy</sub>-onl<sub>y</sub> estimate that would omit this second control.

Section 3 establishes the energy identity, measure-level jump rigidity, and target conver<sub>g</sub>ence<sub>,</sub> followed b<sub>y</sub> the sto<sub>pp</sub>ed far-field estimate. Section 4 constructs the <sub>p</sub>article <sub>p</sub>rocess and <sub>p</sub>roves the mean-field estimate and its conse<sub>q</sub>uences. Section 5 summarizes the results and discusses further questions. Appendix A supplies the positive linear jump-transport equation, product, and em<sub>p</sub>irical sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub> estimates used in the <sub>p</sub>roofs<sub>;</sub> A<sub>pp</sub>endix B dis<sub>p</sub>roves a linear u<sub>pp</sub>er bound on the particle jump comparison remainder in terms of modulated energy and the stated finite-particle correction.

## 2. Well-posedness and stability of the continuum dynamics

Uniqueness follows from a Dobrushin coupling argument [Dob79]: evolve a coupling of the initial laws and estimate its mean separation. We now establish jump-dissipation rigidity for finite-energy measures, using only their normalized first-variation increments. Together with (3.5) in its stated density class, this proves Theorem 1.3. The additional condition $r \geq 2$ is not needed for this <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osition.

Proposition 3.8 (Rigidity of jump dissipation). Assume the target, kernel, proposal, and spatial-rate hypotheses ofTheorem 1.2. Let $\rho \in \mathcal { P } ( \mathbb { R } ^ { d } )$ satisfy $\mathcal { E } _ { q } ( \rho - \mu ) < \infty . \ I f \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { j } } ( \rho ) = 0 ,$ , then $\rho = \mu$

Proof. Since $\mathcal { D } _ { j } ( \boldsymbol { \rho } ) = 0$ and $\lambda ( x ) > 0$ , for �-a.e. �,

$$

$$

Fix such an �. The map $z \mapsto [ \Delta _ { \rho } ( x , z ) ] _ { + }$ is continuous. Since the measure �(�) �� has full support, it follows that $[ \Delta _ { \rho } ( x , z ) ] _ { + } = 0$ for every $z \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d }$ . Using (3.37), we obtain $\widetilde { f } _ { \rho } ( x ) \leq \widetilde { f } _ { \rho } ( x + z )$ for every $z \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d }$ . Thus<sub>,</sub> for $\rho { \cdot } \mathrm { a . e . } \ x , \widetilde f _ { \rho } ( x )$ is the <sub>g</sub>lobal minimum of $\widetilde { f } _ { \rho }$ . Denote this minimum b<sub>y</sub> $m _ { \rho }$ . Then $\widetilde { f } _ { \rho } = m _ { \rho } \rho \mathrm { - a . e . }$ , and $\widetilde { f } _ { \rho } \geq m _ { \rho }$ on $\mathbb { R } ^ { d }$

For $0 < q \leq 1$ <sub>,</sub> the function $\widetilde { f } _ { \rho }$ is globally �-Hölder continuous. For $1 < q < 2$ <sub>,</sub> it is <sub>g</sub>loball<sub>y</sub> Li<sub>p</sub>schitz because $\rho , \mu \in \mathcal P _ { q - 1 } ( \mathbb { R } ^ { d } )$ b<sub>y</sub> Lemma 3.4. Since $\mu \in \mathcal P _ { r } ( \mathbb { R } ^ { d } )$ , where $r \geq \operatorname* { m a x } \{ 1 , q \}$ <sub>,</sub> it follows that $\widetilde f _ { \rho } \in L ^ { 1 } ( \mu )$ . Moreover, $\widetilde { f } _ { \rho } = m _ { \rho } \rho { \mathrm { - a . e . , s o } } \widetilde { f } _ { \rho } \in L ^ { 1 } ( \rho )$ . Hence ${ \widetilde { f } } _ { \rho } \in L ^ { 1 } ( | \rho - \mu | )$ <sub>,</sub> and Lemma 3.7 g<sup>i</sup>ves

$$

4.1. Construction and global noncollision. We construct the process by following the deterministic particle flow until an accepted jump occurs, updating one coordinate, and restarting the flow. This is a piecewise deterministic Markov process in the sense of [Dav84].

## 5. Conclusions and further <sub>q</sub>uestions

We have established global well-posedness and target convergence for the jump-modified energykernel flow under the stated densit<sub>y</sub> and moment h<sub>yp</sub>otheses. Amon<sub>g</sub> Borel <sub>p</sub>robabilit<sub>y</sub> measures with finite energy, the target is the only measure with zerojump dissipation. This allows us to identify narrow limits without a uniform-in-time densit<sub>y</sub> bound. The <sub>p</sub>article construction <sub>p</sub>reserves ener<sub>gy</sub> monotonicit<sub>y p</sub>athwise<sub>,</sub> and the mean-field estimate <sub>g</sub>ives <sub>q</sub>uantitative finite-time a<sub>pp</sub>roximation and joint long-time target approximation. These results leave several questions concerning the continuum theory, quantitative convergence rates, and particle discretization.

The finite critical densit<sub>y</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>onent $p = d / ( d + q - 2 )$ remains outside the continuum construction when $d + q - 2 > 0$ . At this end<sub>p</sub>oint<sub>,</sub> the tar<sub>g</sub>et field $( - \Delta K ) * \mu$ need not be bounded<sub>,</sub> and the densit<sub>y</sub> estimate used here does not close. Extendin<sub>g</sub> the Cauch<sub>y</sub> theor<sub>y</sub> therefore re<sub>q</sub>uires both an appropr<sup>i</sup>a<sup>t</sup>e un<sup>i</sup>queness es<sup>ti</sup>ma<sup>t</sup>e an<sup>d</sup> propaga<sup>ti</sup>on o<sup>f</sup> <sup>th</sup>e cr<sup>iti</sup>ca<sup>l</sup> <sup>d</sup>ens<sup>it</sup>y c<sup>l</sup>ass.

The jump mechanism gives qualitative target convergence, but quantitative rates beyond the stopped far-field regime remain to be determined. One may seek a Łojasiewicz-type estimate of the form

$$
\mathcal { D } ( \rho ) \geq c \mathcal { E } _ { q } ( \rho - \mu ) ^ { 1 + \alpha } , \qquad c > 0 , \quad \alpha \geq 0 ,\tag{5.1}
$$

for the total continuum dissi<sub>p</sub>ation relative to the fixed tar<sub>g</sub>et $\mu .$ One must identif<sub>y</sub> a subclass of the admissible densities in Theorem 1.2<sub>,</sub> with $r \geq 2$ and the tar<sub>g</sub>et<sub>, p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osal<sub>,</sub> and s<sub>p</sub>atial rate fixed<sub>,</sub> that is invariant after entr<sub>y</sub> or contains $\rho _ { t }$ for every $t \geq T _ { 0 }$ , and on which � is uniform. A bound for the entry time $T _ { 0 }$ is also re<sub>q</sub>uired. Combined with the ener<sub>gy</sub> identit<sub>y</sub> in Pro<sub>p</sub>osition 3.2<sub>,</sub> such an estimate would give exponential energy decay for $\alpha = 0$ and al<sub>g</sub>ebraic ener<sub>gy</sub> deca<sub>y</sub> of order $( t - T _ { 0 } ) ^ { - 1 / \alpha }$ as � → ∞ for $\alpha > 0 ;$ ; the MMD rates follow by taking square roots. The size of the jump dissipation near the ordinar<sub>y</sub> flow’s sin<sub>g</sub>ular critical measures is one issue for such an estimate.

For pure transport, the work [RSW26, Theorems 1.7 and 5.23, Proposition 5.21, and Corollary 5.27] g<sup>i</sup>ves compac<sup>t</sup>ness an<sup>d</sup> asymp<sup>t</sup>o<sup>ti</sup>c cr<sup>iti</sup>ca<sup>lit</sup>y <sup>i</sup>n <sup>it</sup>s s<sup>t</sup>a<sup>t</sup>e<sup>d</sup> reg<sup>i</sup>mes. <sup>Th</sup>e rema<sup>i</sup>n<sup>i</sup>ng <sup>t</sup>arge<sup>t</sup>-se<sup>l</sup>ec<sup>ti</sup>on <sub>q</sub>uestion is to determine which h<sub>yp</sub>otheses exclude non-tar<sub>g</sub>et sin<sub>g</sub>ular limits.

A discrete-time <sub>p</sub>article al<sub>g</sub>orithm should account for the sin<sub>g</sub>ular trans<sub>p</sub>ort force and the exact energy cons<sup>t</sup>ra<sup>i</sup>n<sup>t</sup> on accep<sup>t</sup>e<sup>d</sup> moves. <sup>G</sup>enera<sup>l</sup> approx<sup>i</sup>ma<sup>ti</sup>on resu<sup>lt</sup>s <sup>f</sup>or p<sup>i</sup>ecew<sup>i</sup>se-<sup>d</sup>e<sup>t</sup>erm<sup>i</sup>n<sup>i</sup>s<sup>ti</sup>c processes [BBD22] provide a starting point, but do not by themselves give an energy-monotone scheme for this model. A useful objective is a finite-time consistency estimate with explicit dependence on the time ste<sub>p, p</sub>article number<sub>,</sub> and time horizon. Ener<sub>gy</sub> monotonicit<sub>y</sub> could then be combined with such an estimate for long-time target approximation; it does not alone justify exchanging all three limits. Numerical com<sub>p</sub>arisons with trans<sub>p</sub>ort-onl<sub>y</sub> and other MMD methods should use matched com<sub>p</sub>utational costs and inde<sub>p</sub>endent tar<sub>g</sub>et sam<sub>p</sub>les<sub>,</sub> includin<sub>g</sub> in the conditional-<sub>g</sub>eneration and inverse-problem settings of [Hag+24].

$$

For the remainder, the increment of � removes its growth at infinity. More precisely, define

Substituting the convolution into (B.2) and applying Fubini’s theorem now g<sup>i</sup>ves

$$
\begin{array} { l } { \mathcal { R } _ { \rho _ { 0 } } ( \eta - \rho _ { 0 } ) = \displaystyle \iint _ { \mathbb R ^ { 3 } \times \mathbb R ^ { 3 } } H ( x , y ) d ( \eta \otimes \eta ) ( x , y ) - \displaystyle \iint _ { \mathbb R ^ { 3 } \times \mathbb R ^ { 3 } } H ( x , y ) d ( \eta \otimes \rho _ { 0 } ) ( x , y ) } \\ { \displaystyle \qquad - \displaystyle \iint _ { \mathbb R ^ { 3 } \times \mathbb R ^ { 3 } } H ( x , y ) d ( \rho _ { 0 } \otimes \eta ) ( x , y ) + \displaystyle \iint _ { \mathbb R ^ { 3 } \times \mathbb R ^ { 3 } } H ( x , y ) d ( \rho _ { 0 } \otimes \rho _ { 0 } ) ( x , y ) . } \end{array}\tag{B.23}

$\operatorname { I f } \eta _ { n }$ converges narrow<sup>l</sup>y <sup>t</sup>o $\nu ,$ testing with � shows that $\mathcal { R } _ { \rho _ { 0 } } ( \eta _ { n } - \rho _ { 0 } )  \mathcal { R } _ { \rho _ { 0 } } ( \nu - \rho _ { 0 } )$ It remains to construct empirical measures with distinct atoms that converge to � in $W _ { 1 }$ . Let $( Y _ { i } ) _ { i \geq 1 }$ be inde<sub>p</sub>endent random variables with common law $\nu ,$ and set $\begin{array} { r } { \eta _ { N } : = \frac { 1 } { N } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N } \delta _ { Y _ { i } } } \end{array}$ . Uniform a<sub>pp</sub>roximation extends the first conver<sub>g</sub>ence to ever<sub>y</sub> function in $C _ { 0 } ( \mathbb { R } ^ { 3 } )$ . Lemma B.3. Fix $\rho _ { 0 } , \mu \in \mathcal { P } _ { 1 } ( \mathbb { R } ^ { 3 } )$ and $X \in \mathsf { D } _ { N }$ . With $\vartheta _ { 0 } ^ { N } , F _ { N } ( 0 )$ , and $\mathcal { J } _ { 0 } ^ { N }$ defined by (4.33), (4.34), and (4.45), respectively, one has

$$

Proof. Write $F = F _ { N } ( 0 ) , g = g _ { 0 } ^ { N }$ <sub>,</sub> and<sub>,</sub> within this <sub>p</sub>roof<sub>,</sub> set $a ( x , z ) : = f _ { \rho _ { 0 } } ( x ) - f _ { \rho _ { 0 } } ( x + z )$ and $b ( x , z ) : = g ( x ) - g ( x + z )$ . $$

Integrating these bounds shows that the second term in (B.26) lies between $- 4 \mathcal { M } _ { 1 } ( \kappa ) F$ and zero. Proposition B.4 (Failure of an energy-only jump-drift estimate). For the parameters (B.1), there exist strictly positive Schwartz probability densities $\rho _ { 0 } , \mu$ such that

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { \vartheta \in C _ { c } ^ { \infty } ( \mathbb { R } ^ { 3 } ) , \int \vartheta = 0 } \frac { \mathcal { R } _ { \rho _ { 0 } } ( \vartheta ) } { \mathcal { E } _ { 1 } ( \vartheta ) } = \infty .\tag{B.27}
$$

Moreover, there are integers $N _ { k } $ ∞ and configurations $X ^ { ( k ) } \in \mathsf { D } _ { N _ { k } }$ such that

$$

$$

Proof. Choose $\rho _ { 0 } , \mu , \nu _ { k }$ as in Lemma B.1<sub>,</sub> and <sub>p</sub>ut $e _ { k } : = \mathcal { E } _ { 1 } ( \nu _ { k } - \rho _ { 0 } )$ and $r _ { k } : = \mathcal { R } _ { \rho _ { 0 } } ( \nu _ { k } - \rho _ { 0 } ) / e _ { k }$ . Discard finitely many terms so that $r _ { k } > 0$ . For each $k ,$ Lemma B.2 permits us <sup>t</sup>o c<sup>h</sup>oose s<sup>t</sup>r<sup>i</sup>c<sup>tl</sup>y <sup>i</sup>ncreas<sup>i</sup>ng <sup>i</sup>n<sup>t</sup>egers $N _ { k }$ and $X ^ { ( k ) } \in \mathsf { D } _ { N _ { k } }$ with

<sup>A</sup>pp<sup>l</sup>y<sup>i</sup>ng <sup>L</sup>emma <sup>B</sup>.<sup>3</sup> an<sup>d</sup> <sup>di</sup>v<sup>idi</sup>ng <sup>b</sup>y $F _ { N _ { k } } ( 0 ) + N _ { k } ^ { - 1 }$ <sub>,</sub> we conclude that
